PostScript Curve Operators Test Report

Generated on Fri, Apr 4, 2025 2:39:16 PM

Summary

Testing the implementation of PostScript curve operators in the EPS to SVG converter.

Operator: curveto/c

The curveto operator creates a cubic Bezier curve with two control points

Input EPS

%!PS-Adobe-3.0 EPSF-3.0
%%BoundingBox: 0 0 200 200
%%Pages: 1
%%EndComments

% Test file for curveto operator (c)
% This example shows a simple cubic bezier curve using both the full 'curveto' operator
% and the shorthand 'c' operator

% Set line width and colors
1 setlinewidth
0 0 0 setrgbcolor

% First curve using the full 'curveto' operator
newpath
50 50 moveto
70 120 130 120 150 50 curveto
stroke

% Second curve using the abbreviated 'c' operator
newpath
50 150 moveto
70 80 130 80 150 150 c
stroke

% Draw control points for illustration
0 1 0 setrgbcolor  % Green for control points
newpath
70 120 2 0 360 arc fill   % First control point for first curve
130 120 2 0 360 arc fill  % Second control point for first curve

1 0 0 setrgbcolor  % Red for control points
newpath
70 80 2 0 360 arc fill    % First control point for second curve
130 80 2 0 360 arc fill   % Second control point for second curve

% Draw endpoints
0 0 1 setrgbcolor  % Blue for endpoints
newpath
50 50 3 0 360 arc fill    % Starting point of first curve
150 50 3 0 360 arc fill   % End point of first curve
50 150 3 0 360 arc fill   % Starting point of second curve
150 150 3 0 360 arc fill  % End point of second curve

% Add labels
/Helvetica findfont 8 scalefont setfont
0 0 0 setrgbcolor  % Black for text

50 40 moveto (Start) show
150 40 moveto (End) show
75 120 moveto (Control 1) show
125 120 moveto (Control 2) show
60 155 moveto (curveto test) show

showpage
%%EOF

Output SVG

Path Validation

  • First curve (curveto) path coordinates match expected values (100% accuracy)
  • Second curve (c) path coordinates match expected values (100% accuracy)

Summary: 2/2 validations passed

Operator: v

The v operator creates a curve using the current point as the first control point

Input EPS

%!PS-Adobe-3.0 EPSF-3.0
%%BoundingBox: 0 0 200 200
%%Pages: 1
%%EndComments

% Test file for 'v' operator
% The 'v' operator creates a curve using the current point as the first control point
% Syntax: x2 y2 x3 y3 v (where current point is x1,y1)

% Set line width and colors
1 setlinewidth
0 0 0 setrgbcolor

% Draw a curve using the 'v' operator
newpath
50 100 moveto  % This point becomes the implicit first control point
80 150 150 100 v  % Second control point (80,150) and endpoint (150,100)
stroke

% For comparison, draw a similar curve using the regular curveto
newpath
50 50 moveto
50 50 80 0 150 50 curveto  % Note the first control point is the same as current point
stroke

% Draw control points for illustration
0 1 0 setrgbcolor  % Green for control points
newpath
50 100 2 0 360 arc fill   % First control point for v curve (same as start point)
80 150 2 0 360 arc fill   % Second control point for v curve

1 0 0 setrgbcolor  % Red for control points
newpath
50 50 2 0 360 arc fill    % First control point for curveto curve (same as start point)
80 0 2 0 360 arc fill     % Second control point for curveto curve

% Draw endpoints
0 0 1 setrgbcolor  % Blue for endpoints
newpath
50 100 3 0 360 arc fill   % Starting point of v curve
150 100 3 0 360 arc fill  % End point of v curve
50 50 3 0 360 arc fill    % Starting point of curveto curve
150 50 3 0 360 arc fill   % End point of curveto curve

% Draw lines connecting control points
0.5 setgray
newpath
50 100 moveto  % Start at first control point (v curve)
80 150 lineto   % Line to second control point (v curve)
150 100 lineto  % Line to endpoint (v curve)
stroke

newpath
50 50 moveto   % Start at first control point (curveto curve)
80 0 lineto    % Line to second control point (curveto curve)
150 50 lineto  % Line to endpoint (curveto curve)
stroke

% Add labels
/Helvetica findfont 8 scalefont setfont
0 0 0 setrgbcolor  % Black for text

45 110 moveto (Start/CP1) show
80 160 moveto (CP2) show
150 110 moveto (End) show
60 30 moveto (Regular curveto) show
60 120 moveto (v operator) show

showpage
%%EOF

Output SVG

  • Comparison curve path matches expected values (100% accuracy)

Operator: y

The y operator creates a curve using the endpoint as the second control point

Input EPS

%!PS-Adobe-3.0 EPSF-3.0
%%BoundingBox: 0 0 200 200
%%Pages: 1
%%EndComments

% Test file for 'y' operator
% The 'y' operator creates a curve using the endpoint as the second control point
% Syntax: x1 y1 x3 y3 y (where x2,y2 = x3,y3)

% Set line width and colors
1 setlinewidth
0 0 0 setrgbcolor

% Draw a curve using the 'y' operator
newpath
50 100 moveto  % Starting point
100 150 150 100 y  % First control point (100,150) and endpoint (150,100) 
                   % The endpoint is also used as the second control point
stroke

% For comparison, draw a similar curve using the regular curveto
newpath
50 50 moveto
100 0 150 50 150 50 curveto  % Note the second control point is the same as the endpoint
stroke

% Draw control points for illustration
0 1 0 setrgbcolor  % Green for control points (y curve)
newpath
100 150 2 0 360 arc fill   % First control point for y curve
150 100 2 0 360 arc fill   % Second control point for y curve (same as endpoint)

1 0 0 setrgbcolor  % Red for control points (curveto curve)
newpath
100 0 2 0 360 arc fill     % First control point for curveto curve
150 50 2 0 360 arc fill    % Second control point for curveto curve (same as endpoint)

% Draw endpoints
0 0 1 setrgbcolor  % Blue for endpoints
newpath
50 100 3 0 360 arc fill    % Starting point of y curve
150 100 3 0 360 arc fill   % End point of y curve
50 50 3 0 360 arc fill     % Starting point of curveto curve
150 50 3 0 360 arc fill    % End point of curveto curve

% Draw lines connecting control points
0.5 setgray
newpath
50 100 moveto   % Start point (y curve)
100 150 lineto  % Line to first control point (y curve)
150 100 lineto  % Line to second control point/endpoint (y curve)
stroke

newpath
50 50 moveto    % Start point (curveto curve)
100 0 lineto    % Line to first control point (curveto curve)
150 50 lineto   % Line to second control point/endpoint (curveto curve)
stroke

% Add labels
/Helvetica findfont 8 scalefont setfont
0 0 0 setrgbcolor  % Black for text

45 110 moveto (Start) show
100 160 moveto (CP1) show
155 110 moveto (End/CP2) show
60 30 moveto (Regular curveto) show
60 120 moveto (y operator) show

showpage
%%EOF

Operator: arc

The arc operator creates a circular arc

Input EPS

%!PS-Adobe-3.0 EPSF-3.0
%%BoundingBox: 0 0 200 200
%%Pages: 1
%%EndComments

% Test file for 'arc' operator
% The 'arc' operator creates a circular arc
% Syntax: x y r start_angle end_angle arc

% Set line width and colors
1 setlinewidth
0 0 0 setrgbcolor

% Draw a full circle using the arc operator
newpath
100 100 40 0 360 arc
stroke

% Draw a 90-degree arc
newpath
100 100 30 0 90 arc
stroke

% Draw a 180-degree arc
newpath
100 100 20 0 180 arc
stroke

% Draw a 270-degree arc
newpath
100 100 10 0 270 arc
stroke

% Draw arcs with different starting angles
% 45-degree arc starting at 45 degrees
0 0 1 setrgbcolor  % Blue for this arc
newpath
100 100 50 45 90 arc
stroke

% 90-degree arc starting at 135 degrees
1 0 0 setrgbcolor  % Red for this arc
newpath
100 100 50 135 225 arc
stroke

% 90-degree arc starting at 270 degrees
0 1 0 setrgbcolor  % Green for this arc
newpath
100 100 50 270 360 arc
stroke

% Mark the center point
0 0 0 setrgbcolor  % Black
newpath
100 100 2 0 360 arc fill

% Add radius lines to show angles
0.7 setgray
newpath
100 100 moveto
140 100 lineto  % 0 degrees
stroke

newpath
100 100 moveto
100 140 lineto  % 90 degrees
stroke

newpath
100 100 moveto
60 100 lineto   % 180 degrees
stroke

newpath
100 100 moveto
100 60 lineto   % 270 degrees
stroke

% Add labels
/Helvetica findfont 8 scalefont setfont
0 0 0 setrgbcolor  % Black for text

142 100 moveto (0°) show
102 142 moveto (90°) show
50 100 moveto (180°) show
92 58 moveto (270°) show
100 30 moveto (arc operator test) show

showpage
%%EOF

Result: PASS

Operator: arcn

The arcn operator creates a counterclockwise circular arc

Input EPS

%!PS-Adobe-3.0 EPSF-3.0
%%BoundingBox: 0 0 200 200
%%Pages: 1
%%EndComments

% Test file for 'arcn' operator
% The 'arcn' operator creates a circular arc in the counterclockwise direction
% Syntax: x y r start_angle end_angle arcn
% Note: arcn draws in the opposite direction from arc

% Set line width and colors
1 setlinewidth
0 0 0 setrgbcolor

% Draw a full circle using the arcn operator
newpath
100 100 40 0 0 arcn  % Start and end at 0 degrees, go full circle counterclockwise
stroke

% Draw a 90-degree arcn (counterclockwise from 90 to 0 degrees)
newpath
100 100 30 90 0 arcn
stroke

% Draw a 180-degree arcn (counterclockwise from 180 to 0 degrees)
newpath
100 100 20 180 0 arcn
stroke

% Draw a 270-degree arcn (counterclockwise from 270 to 0 degrees)
newpath
100 100 10 270 0 arcn
stroke

% Draw arcs with different starting angles
% 45-degree arc starting at 90 degrees (counterclockwise to 45 degrees)
0 0 1 setrgbcolor  % Blue for this arc
newpath
100 100 50 90 45 arcn
stroke

% 90-degree arc starting at 225 degrees (counterclockwise to 135 degrees)
1 0 0 setrgbcolor  % Red for this arc
newpath
100 100 50 225 135 arcn
stroke

% 90-degree arc starting at 360 degrees (counterclockwise to 270 degrees)
0 1 0 setrgbcolor  % Green for this arc
newpath
100 100 50 360 270 arcn
stroke

% For comparison, draw one arc using both arc and arcn
0.5 0 0.5 setrgbcolor  % Purple
newpath
150 150 20 0 90 arc  % Clockwise from 0 to 90 degrees
stroke

0 0.5 0.5 setrgbcolor  % Teal
newpath
150 150 15 90 0 arcn  % Counterclockwise from 90 to 0 degrees
stroke

% Mark the center points
0 0 0 setrgbcolor  % Black
newpath
100 100 2 0 360 arc fill
150 150 2 0 360 arc fill

% Add radius lines to show angles
0.7 setgray
newpath
100 100 moveto
140 100 lineto  % 0 degrees
stroke

newpath
100 100 moveto
100 140 lineto  % 90 degrees
stroke

newpath
100 100 moveto
60 100 lineto   % 180 degrees
stroke

newpath
100 100 moveto
100 60 lineto   % 270 degrees
stroke

% Add labels
/Helvetica findfont 8 scalefont setfont
0 0 0 setrgbcolor  % Black for text

142 100 moveto (0°) show
102 142 moveto (90°) show
50 100 moveto (180°) show
92 58 moveto (270°) show
100 30 moveto (arcn operator test) show
150 130 moveto (arc vs arcn) show

showpage
%%EOF

Output SVG

  • Full circle arcn path found
  • 90-degree arcn path has correct direction (counterclockwise)
  • Comparison arcn path found (with correct direction)

Operator: arct

The arct operator creates a tangent arc between two lines

Input EPS

%!PS-Adobe-3.0 EPSF-3.0
%%BoundingBox: 0 0 200 200
%%Pages: 1
%%EndComments

% Test file for 'arct' operator
% The 'arct' operator creates a tangent arc between two lines
% Syntax: x1 y1 x2 y2 r arct
% This draws an arc of radius r tangent to the lines (current point to x1,y1) and (x1,y1 to x2,y2)

% Set line width and colors
1 setlinewidth
0 0 0 setrgbcolor

% Example 1: 90-degree corner with rounded edges
newpath
50 50 moveto       % Starting point
50 100 100 100 10 arct  % Round the corner with a 10-unit radius
100 100 lineto    % Complete the path
stroke

% Example 2: Different radius
newpath
50 150 moveto       % Starting point
50 120 100 120 20 arct  % Round the corner with a 20-unit radius
100 120 lineto    % Complete the path
stroke

% Example 3: Multiple arct corners to create a rounded rectangle
0 0 1 setrgbcolor  % Blue
newpath
120 50 moveto       % Starting point
170 50 lineto      % Bottom edge
170 50 170 100 10 arct  % Bottom-right corner
170 100 lineto     % Right edge
170 100 120 100 10 arct  % Top-right corner
120 100 lineto     % Top edge
120 100 120 50 10 arct   % Top-left corner
stroke

% Example 4: Acute angle with rounded corner
1 0 0 setrgbcolor  % Red
newpath
120 150 moveto     % Starting point
150 120 170 150 15 arct  % Round the acute angle with a 15-unit radius
170 150 lineto     % Complete the path
stroke

% Example 5: Obtuse angle with rounded corner
0 1 0 setrgbcolor  % Green
newpath
120 180 moveto     % Starting point
140 170 120 140 8 arct   % Round the obtuse angle with a 8-unit radius
120 140 lineto     % Complete the path
stroke

% Mark the tangent points and centers for Example 1
0.7 setgray
newpath
50 90 2 0 360 arc fill  % First tangent point
90 100 2 0 360 arc fill % Second tangent point
80 90 2 0 360 arc fill  % Center of the arc

% Add dotted lines to show the original sharp corner for Example 1
[2 2] 0 setdash
newpath
50 50 moveto
50 100 lineto
100 100 lineto
stroke
[] 0 setdash

% Add labels
/Helvetica findfont 8 scalefont setfont
0 0 0 setrgbcolor  % Black for text

55 70 moveto (r=10) show
55 135 moveto (r=20) show
140 75 moveto (Rounded rectangle) show
150 130 moveto (Acute angle) show
130 160 moveto (Obtuse angle) show
75 30 moveto (arct operator test) show

showpage
